### 53. What a datacenter pays, the All grids tab: Texas rows: the utility's own transmission rate

- **Page:** What a datacenter pays, the All grids tab, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/cost-of-power?view=allgrids
- **Question:** A Texas row's all-in figure uses the Texas commission's transmission rate for every wires utility. Where the row's own utility is known, should it use that utility's own rate?
- **Now:** The commission's rate on every Texas row. On the page as built on 17 November (a 100 MW datacenter at a 90 percent load factor): USD 8.62 per MWh of transmission on every Texas row.
- **Other choice:** The three rows whose utility is known take its own rate. The two Houston rows (CenterPoint): USD 6.92 per MWh, all-in 42.52 and 42.83. The Austin row (Oncor): USD 8.58, all-in 47.59. Every other row unchanged.
- **Change:** The Houston rows' transmission part -1.70, -19.8 percent (all-in about -3.9 percent); the Oncor row -0.04, -0.5 percent.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-cost-of-power-3](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-cost-of-power-3)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [cost-of-power-3-texas-transmission-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/cost-of-power-3-texas-transmission-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No recommendation: the measurement and the build give this figure differently (see the foot). To weigh once that is settled: a utility's own rate is the one its customer pays; only three Texas rows have a known utility, so the tab would mix two rules.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, datacenter cost](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-cost-of-power-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, datacenter cost](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-cost-of-power-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** One publisher read; the rule's own text could not be read. The Independent Market Monitor for ERCOT (Potomac Economics), "2021 State of the Market Report for the ERCOT Electricity Markets" (https://www.potomaceconomics.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/2021-State-of-the-Market-Report.pdf), PDF pages 28 and 68 of 211: transmission cost in Texas is shared out by each load's part of the highest 15-minute demand of June, July, August and September (the four coincident peaks), one method for the whole grid; the report gives no rate by utility. The Public Utility Commission of Texas's rule on transmission rates (16 TAC 25.192) and the utilities' tariffs were not read today: the Commission's site answered HTTP 402 to the rule's address, and ERCOT's site could not be reached. So which rate a published all-in figure for a Texas site uses is not read today.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** His ruling 1a of 18 October 2026 (`docs/methods/datacenter_pays_algorithm.md`, "Session 298") left the four utilities' delivery table on "Your load" as built: each utility's own lines in a table of their own. It does not reach the All grids tab's all-in figure, which adds a transmission part by the tab's own rule. None found on that figure (searched the two rulings files, the roadmap and the page standard for transmission, all-in and postage).
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/siting.md`, version 14: the Texas rows' hover already names the four wires utilities' own lines, and the all-in figure "still counts the Commission's rate", marked there as waiting for a ruling. `docs/methods/datacenter_cost.md` ("The Commission's transmission charge matrices") and `datacenter_pays_algorithm.md` say what each rate is: the Commission's rate is the wholesale charge every wires utility pays, and a retail customer pays it through its own utility's transmission factor. That speaks for the utility's own rate where the utility is truly the site's. Against the branch as built: it gives the Austin zone's row Oncor's rate, matched by the county's delivery-only company, while the page's own file (`site/data/siting/utility.json`) names Austin Energy, a city utility with no tariff among the four, as that zone's seller; CenterPoint's rate is a charge per kVA counted as per kW; and the four tariff files carry their own dates beside a Commission rate approved on 5 November 2026. The figures of the foot are settled: the build's (8.62, 6.92, 8.58 USD per MWh) are at the 2026 rate the page now reads.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. The method notes say a customer pays through its own utility's factor, which speaks for the other choice, but the Commission's rule could not be read today and the branch gives the Austin row a utility that the page's own file does not name as its seller, so the evidence does not decide.

`Ruling:`

### 55. Where power contracts are being struck: Count the opening view by contract and merged buyer names

- **Page:** Where power contracts are being struck, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/contracts
- **Question:** The opening view's sentence counts agreements by the seller's name and buyers by their names as filed. The page's other three views count contracts by the filer's identifier and merge a buyer's spellings. Should the opening view count the same way?
- **Now:** "174 agreements between 78 sellers and 95 buyers" (the second quarter of 2026). The first quarter: 243, 102 and 161. The fourth quarter of 2025: 213, 134 and 143.
- **Other choice:** "175 contracts between 78 sellers and 86 buyers". The first quarter: 243, 102 and 136. The fourth quarter of 2025: 214, 134 and 135. Only the buyers move much: spellings of one buyer are counted once.
- **Change:** Buyers -9.5 percent (second quarter of 2026), -15.5 percent (first quarter), -5.6 percent (fourth quarter of 2025); contracts +1; sellers 0.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-contracts-4](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-contracts-4). On the site it needs a database migration applied by a person and a load by the data machine.
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. The method note records the two ways of counting as a decision of the page, an agreement in the list and a contract in the other three views, so it does not prefer one. To weigh: one page counts buyers two ways, and the merged count counts 8 to 25 fewer buyers a quarter; the other choice needs a migration and a load.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, contracts](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-contracts-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, contracts](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-contracts-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** FERC, Electric Quarterly Report Data Dictionary, Version 3.5, issued 23 November 2020 (https://www.ferc.gov/sites/default/files/2020-11/Data_Dictionary_V3_5_Clean.pdf, Contract Data, page 4; whether a later version is in force was not read today), bears on the two halves of the question in opposite ways. Field 20, the service agreement identifier, is the form's name for the agreement itself, the identifier the seller can use "to produce the agreement, if requested"; Field 15, the contract unique identifier, only designates a record of the filing. That is the opening view's unit, not the other three views'. On buyers the form gives nothing to count by: Field 17, the customer's name, is free text of up to 70 characters, and FERC publishes no identifier for a buyer and no rule for joining spellings. No second publisher of these filings was read.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found. Searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the "Rulings" of `docs/roadmap.md` and the page standard for agreement, contract, buyer, spelling and name. The page standard does not say that two views of one page must count alike.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/contracts.md` keeps both ways on purpose. Section 3: in the list an agreement is a distinct pair of seller name and service agreement identifier, "not the contract of sections 5 to 7", and sellers and buyers are the distinct names as filed. Decision 6: two counts with two names. Decision 9: in the other three views a buyer's spellings are counted together only when six rules leave them identical, and a buyer's count is a floor.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. FERC's dictionary favours the opening view's unit for an agreement and is silent on how to count buyers, which is the only figure that moves much; the method note records both counts as its decision, and no ruling says one page must count one way, so nothing found picks a side (looked for a FERC identifier or rule for a buyer: there is none).

`Ruling:`

Should a whole month's average always come from the monthly table?

The nearest ruling is the one the rulings file of 11 October 2026 records for the battery page, "counts whole months only": a month's figure stands on the whole month. The whole-month figure is also confirmed inside the warehouse: the price board's 31 daily averages give 46.78 and a third table's 29 days 46.72, while 41.94 covers 471 of 744 hours.

### 57. The deal tracker, the counts by grid: Read a deal's grid from the story before the state

- **Page:** The deal tracker, the counts by grid, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/deals
- **Question:** Three views of the tracker give a deal its grid from its state alone. The table's own Grid column first takes a grid the story names. Should the three views do the same?
- **Now:** The state alone: grid not stated 544 deals, PJM 10.
- **Other choice:** The story's grid first: not stated 543, PJM 11.
- **Change:** One deal moves: PJM +1, +10 percent of its count.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-deals-3](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-deals-3). Five older tests that hold main's reading are left failing on the branch; they would move with the ruling.
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [deals-3-grid-held-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/deals-3-grid-held-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: one page reads a deal's grid two ways today; the change moves one deal.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, deal tracker](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-deals-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, deal tracker](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-deals-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** Not found. The two public trackers read do not say how a deal is given a grid: the Clean Energy Buyers Association's Deal Tracker (https://ceba.org/deal-tracker/, the inclusion criteria; the last quarter of that long page was not read) and the Clean Investment Monitor (https://www.cleaninvestmentmonitor.org/methodology). So published practice does not decide it.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** The human ruling at the head of `archive/sessions/SESSION_16_PROMPT.md` (late September 2026): the extractor must not infer state, country or status beyond the stated words, and empty is correct. The like reading here: a grid the story names is the stated word, and a grid read from a state is the ERW's inference; the three views use the inference and pass over the stated word. Nothing on a deal's grid in `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's rulings or the page standard.
  - **The tool's own method note:** The notes give the table's rule and record the three views as the place that departs from it. `docs/methods/energy_deals.md`, "Each field": `grid` is kept where the story names the operator, and is never read from a state in the table (the page reads a state's main grid, and says so). `docs/methods/deal_tracker.md`, section 6 and decision 7: the table's own Grid column reads a grid a story names first, then the state; the tracker tab, the comparables and the AI view read the state alone; section 12, item 4, lists that difference as waiting for the owner. One row differs today.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **switch**. Samuel's ruling of session 16 decided it: what a story's own words state comes before anything inferred, and the table's rule already puts a grid the story names first, so the three views should read it before the state as the Grid column does.

### 58. Policy monitor, the large-load laws: Keep Michigan's tax exemption among the large-load laws

- **Page:** Policy monitor, the large-load laws, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/policy?view=monitor#large-load-laws
- **Question:** Michigan's one law in the table of state laws on large loads is a sales tax exemption for data centers that names no smallest load. Should it stay in the table?
- **Now:** 27 laws and bills, 10 of them in effect, 26 states with a law. Michigan's row is Enrolled House Bill 4906 (Public Act 207 of 2024).
- **Other choice:** 26 laws and bills, 9 in effect, 25 states. Michigan's row leaves, and the All grids tab no longer links Michigan to a law.
- **Change:** Laws shown -1, -3.7 percent; laws in effect -1, -10.0 percent.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-policy-2](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-policy-2)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [policy-2-michigan-law-out-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/policy-2-michigan-law-out-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. The table's scope is not written down as a rule that settles this row. To weigh: taking Michigan out is a change of scope (laws that set terms for a large load's connection or rates, not tax exemptions) that may take other rows with it; on the file as built no other law is taken out.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, policy monitor](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-policy-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, policy monitor](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-policy-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** Not read today. The publisher a reader would know for state laws on data centers is the National Conference of State Legislatures; its robots file disallows this reader on every path, so no page of it was asked for and no claim is taken from it. No other tracker of state large-load laws that publishes its scope was found by search. So published practice does not decide it.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on the scope of the laws table. Searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the "Rulings" of `docs/roadmap.md` and the page standard for law, tax, exemption, Michigan and scope. The chain file of 16 November 2026 (goal 1) asks for the state laws on `/policy` and does not define them. The page standard's line that an upgrade "drops nothing the page had" bears against taking a row out.
  - **The tool's own method note:** The scope is written down, in the research's own method that `docs/methods/large_load_research.md` names: `docs/research/large_load/README.md`, "The third pass": one entry a state law or bill of the 2025 and 2026 sessions that sets terms for large loads (data centers and others of roughly 20 MW and up) or tells the utilities or the commission to set them; its list of topics includes "siting or tax". Michigan's row (`docs/research/large_load/legislation_2026-11-13.csv`) is not a bare exemption: the act (Public Act 207 of 2024, section 4(e)(ix) and (x)) makes the exemption depend on the data center procuring clean energy for 90 percent of its use, which it may show by a long-term contract with its utility under which no cost of serving it is passed to other customers; bars it from three kinds of rate, one of them any rate that has residential customers subsidise it; and tells the utilities to develop tariffs and contracts for this. It names no size in megawatts; it defines the data center by an investment of USD 250 million. One thing in the row sits outside the written scope: it is a 2024-session act, approved 17 January 2025 and in effect 17 April 2025, which the row's own note says.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **keep**. The research's written scope decided it: a law that sets terms for large loads or tells the utilities to set them is in the table, and Michigan's act does both through its contract and rate conditions, so it is there for those terms and not for the tax exemption alone; the one point against, that it is a 2024-session act approved and in effect in 2025, is not the question asked.

`Ruling:`

### 59. The weekday Digest, the line on national demand: The Digest's "yesterday" as a US day, not the UTC day

- **Page:** The weekday Digest, the line on national demand, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/digest
- **Question:** The Digest's line "US48 demand, yesterday: peaked at ..." takes yesterday as the day in UTC, which starts at 7 or 8 p.m. Eastern, in the middle of the evening peak. Should yesterday be a US day?
- **Now:** The UTC day. The issue of 18 October gave 495,806 MW as the peak of 17 October; the issue of 17 October gave 540,657 MW.
- **Other choice:** The Eastern day: 448,936 MW for 17 October; 514,073 MW for 16 October. Over the 33 days measured the peak moves on 14, each time because the UTC day's highest hour is the evening peak of the US day before. The change against the week before moves on 12 of 26 days and turns sign on 3 (24 October: -4.8 percent to +2.6 percent).
- **Change:** The largest: -46,870 MW, -9.45 percent (17 October); on the 14 days that move, -2.29 percent on average; the peak never rises.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-site-digest-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-site-digest-1)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides which day the Digest means. To weigh: on 14 of 33 days the line gives as yesterday's peak the evening of the day before; the Digest is paused this week and the owner's rules say its words do not change now, so a ruling would take effect when it resumes; which US day (Eastern here) and whether the hour is written in that clock are part of it.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, the Digest](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-site-digest-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branch, the Digest](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-site-digest-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** Two publishers, and they agree: a day's peak is given on a US clock day, never the UTC day. EIA, Today in Energy, "U.S. electricity peak demand set new records twice in July" (5 August 2025, https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/detail.php?id=65864): the Lower 48's peak of 28 July 2025 is dated by its US day and its hour is given as 6:00 to 7:00 p.m. "eastern time", from the Hourly Electric Grid Monitor. ERCOT, Monthly Operational Overview (August 2026), 16 September 2026 (https://www.ercot.com/files/docs/2026/09/16/ERCOT-Monthly-Operational-Overview-August-2026.pdf), page 2: the month's peak is at "hour ending 18" on a Central date. The warehouse's own notes on EIA's daily tables say the same of EIA's daily figures: the day is EIA's Eastern day for every area, "not a UTC day" (`docs/methods/eia930_daily_demand.md`); EIA's own page stating this was not read today (the monitor's "About" page is drawn by script).
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** His rulings of 24 October 2026 on the periodicals (`docs/brand.md`, section 10, "The Daily Digest"): the Digest's masthead gives its data cutoff as a time in Eastern Time with the UTC time beside it ("Data through 11:45 ET, 15:45 UTC"). So the clock the Digest states its times in is ruled: Eastern, with UTC beside it. (The send time, 09:00 Eastern in the brandbook, is still listed as open there.) Also in force: the owner's rule 5 of 16 November 2026 (`queue/done/302-day-16-november-oldlaptop-large-load-on-the-site.md`): the Digest's words do not change while the news is paused, so a switch would take effect when he resumes it. Nothing on the demand line's day in `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md` or the roadmap's "Rulings" (searched: Digest, yesterday, UTC, Eastern, day).
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/digest_scoring.md`, "The numbers block": the physical row is the Lower 48's peak hour against the same weekday a year or a week before; it does not say which day "yesterday" is. The code does (`warehouse/news/brief.py`): `demand_lines` takes the UTC day before the issue, while the same issue's price lines take each grid's local operating day (`dam_mean`, and the real-time maximum), and the real-time line writes its hour in the grid's zone with UTC beside it. `docs/methods/demand_growth.md` reads the Lower 48's years, months and hours of the day on the Eastern clock. The demand line is the one line of the Digest on the UTC day.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **switch**. Samuel's ruling of 24 October 2026 that the Digest states its times in Eastern Time with UTC beside it decides the clock, and EIA's and ERCOT's practice and the Digest's own price lines all date a day's peak on a US clock day; it changes no issue until he lifts the pause on the Digest's words.

`Ruling:`

### 61. What is on an electricity bill, the calculator: Weight the wholesale price over a meter-read span

- **Page:** What is on an electricity bill, the calculator, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/learn/bill
- **Question:** A bill covers the days between two meter reads, which seldom match a calendar month. Should the calculator let the reader give the read days and weight the wholesale price by the days of each month the bill covers?
- **Now:** One calendar month: September 2026. Northern California USD 44.85 per MWh (600 kWh's wholesale USD 26.91); the Texas hub 39.50 (23.70); Southern California 38.52 (23.11).
- **Other choice:** Two optional read days. A read from 16 September to 15 October 2026 (an example, 15 days of each month): Northern California USD 47.48 (the bar USD 28.49), the Texas hub 41.06 (24.64), Southern California 38.50 (23.10). With no days given the bar reads one month, as now. The five cards do not move.
- **Change:** On the example span: +5.9 percent (Northern California), +4.0 percent (Texas), -0.04 percent (Southern California).
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-bill-3](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-bill-3)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: it changes nothing unless the reader types the days; it adds two fields to a page meant to teach a first-time reader.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, electricity bill](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-bill-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, electricity bill](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-bill-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** The publishers do not make this choice: no utility and no EIA table sets a wholesale price against a customer's bill, so none shows over which days it should be averaged. What was read shows only how a bill's own period works. Pacific Gas and Electric, Electric Rule No. 9, "Rendering and Payment of Bills" (https://www.pge.com/tariffs/assets/pdf/tariffbook/ELEC_RULES_9.pdf, Sheet 1, section A, effective 4 November 2017): a bill runs from one meter reading to the next, not a calendar month; a period of 27 to 33 days counts as a month, and where daily quantities are used the bill takes "the number of days in the billing period". EIA's retail price tables (Electric Power Monthly, Table 5.1 read today, https://www.eia.gov/electricity/monthly/epm_table_grapher.php?t=epmt_5_01) are by calendar month. Southern California Edison's and San Diego Gas and Electric's rules were not read today.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on the bill page's wholesale month (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's "Rulings" and the page standard for: bill, meter, month, wholesale, days). Near it, and not deciding: the roadmap ruling of 12 October 2026 that every assumption is editable in place, made for the three cost tools; the page standard of 16 November 2026 asks for a dense, usable page and says nothing on adding fields to a teaching page.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/bill.md`, "The wholesale share" and the decision `wholesale-month`: the price is the latest month whose real-time hours are all held, else the latest month held, marked partial. The note already takes the bill's days as an input (30 by default) for the tariff's own daily quantities. The other choice keeps this default when no read days are typed, so the note's rule stands under both choices and does not say whether the two fields are added.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. Looked for and not found: a publisher that weights a wholesale price over a bill's meter-read span, or a ruling or method rule on adding optional fields to this page; the tariff shows only that a real bill follows read days, which makes the other choice true to a bill without saying the page should carry it.

`Ruling:`

### 62. The Flex Alert scorecard: Keep more decimals so two cells round once

- **Page:** The Flex Alert scorecard, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/grid/caiso/alerts
- **Question:** Two cells are rounded twice (once when the table is built, again on the page) and sit one off in their last digit. Should the builder keep more decimals so the page rounds once?
- **Now:** Four decimals stored. The alert day of 29 July 2021 reads -0.18 percent (the table holds -0.175, of -0.174972). The year 2024 reads -16,893.33 MWh (the table holds -16,893.325).
- **Other choice:** Six decimals stored: 29 July 2021 reads -0.17 percent; 2024 reads -16,893.32 MWh at the builder's next run. No other cell of the 471 on the page moves.
- **Change:** Two cells move by one in the last digit shown (-0.18 to -0.17 is 5.6 percent of that cell).
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-caiso-alerts-2](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-caiso-alerts-2)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground is needed to see which digit is right, but none of the four names it: the underlying figure, -0.174972, rounds to -0.17, and the page shows -0.18 only because it rounds a figure that was already rounded. To weigh: the fix is a builder change for the data machine and touches two cells.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, Flex Alert scorecard](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-caiso-alerts-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branch, Flex Alert scorecard](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-caiso-alerts-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** One publisher read, and it states the rule outright. EIA, Electric Power Monthly, Table ES1.A, "Total Electric Power Industry Summary Statistics, 2026 and 2025" (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/monthly/epm_table_grapher.php?t=table_es1a), the notes under the table: "Percentage change is calculated before rounding." EIA computes a printed figure from the unrounded values and rounds once, for print. No second publisher's rounding rule was read today (CAISO's and ERCOT's pages read today state none).
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on rounding or stored decimals (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's "Rulings", the page standard and `docs/brand.md` for: round, decimal, digit). Near it: the non-negotiable that every number traces to its source (`CLAUDE.md`, rule 5), and the data standard's rule that derived monthly sums use exact decimal arithmetic (`docs/datastandard.md`, the decision on derived monthly sums).
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/caiso_alerts.md`, section 4, "What is recomputed, and what was found": the page's figures are held to the tables "inside half the page's last shown digit"; 467 of 471 agree, and these two cells are among the four it names as differences, each put down to the same double rounding: 29 July 2021 prints -0.18 where the hours give -0.17497, and 2024 prints -16,893.33 where the hours give -16,893.3249. The note treats both as faults of the builder's stored decimals (`warehouse/derived/flex_alert_scorecard.py` writes four), not as a choice.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **switch**. The method note decides it: by its own test (half the last shown digit against the hours) the two cells are named faults of rounding twice, and EIA's stated practice is the same, a figure computed before rounding and rounded once; the change is the data machine's, at the builder's next run.

`Ruling:`

- **Question:** The wholesale share of each bill counts the kWh on the meter. About 5 percent more is bought at wholesale and lost on the wires. Should the share count those losses? - **Branch:** [wip/alt-bill-4](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-bill-4). The losses half only; the Texas zone half is not built.
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **keep**. The page standard says every figure carries its source document, and the 5 percent is the question's own round figure: no utility's published loss factor is held. Once one is read for each utility, the losses can be counted with a source.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, electricity bill](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-bill-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, electricity bill](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-bill-2026-11-17.md)

`Ruling:`

### 64. The project map, the queue and planned layers: Match planned plants and queue requests so a project counts once

- **Page:** The project map, the queue and planned layers, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/map
- **Question:** A project can be on the map twice: as a planned plant in EIA's list and as a request in a connection queue. Should the two be matched by name and county, so such a project counts once?
- **Now:** Both rows stay, each kind with its own sum: 3,684 queue projects (768,296.0 MW) and 2,361 planned units (290,264.2 MW).
- **Other choice:** Matched by name (without words such as Solar, BESS, LLC) and county: 154 queue projects match a planned unit, 30,170.5 MW, and leave the queue layer's count and sum. Three projects near Abilene that the rulings list names are among them (351.1, 250.9 and 156.0 MW). On the builder's own copy of the tables the match finds 163 projects and 31,574.5 MW.
- **Change:** The queue layer -154 projects, -30,170.5 MW, -3.93 percent of its megawatts.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-map-2](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-map-2)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: the two layers together now count about 30 GW twice; a match by words can miss a project and can pair two different ones, and the rule of the match would be part of the ruling.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, project map](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-map-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, project map](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-map-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** Berkeley Lab, "Queued Up: 2026 Edition" (the slide deck, https://eta-publications.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2026-06/queued_up_2026_edition.pdf, slide 34 and its note 2), sets the capacity its queue data shows as operating beside EIA's capacity as two sources, and explains the gap between them in words (missing in-service dates, lags and gaps in the queues' reporting); it does not match a queue request to an EIA plant, and the deck gives no rule for doing so. EIA's Form EIA-860 data page (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860/) describes its files by plant and generator as the respondents report them and names no queue. So the publishers keep the two lists apart; neither publishes a matched list.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on matching the two lists (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's rulings and the page standard for match, twice, double and count once). The nearest is the human ruling at the head of `archive/sessions/SESSION_16_PROMPT.md` (late September 2026) that nothing is inferred beyond the stated words: a match by the words of a name and a county is an inference that two rows are one project, which neither source states.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/map.md`, the decisions table, states the rule and the reason, and the reason is this overlap. "Megawatts are never added across kinds": each kind has its own count and sum, because a queue position is a request and most are withdrawn before they are built. "Which queue positions are on the map": a completed position may be a unit EIA's inventory already lists, "which is one reason megawatts are not added across kinds". "Where a queue position is drawn": no fuzzy matching.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **keep**. The method note decided it: the page already answers a project standing in both lists by never adding megawatts across kinds, it names that overlap as the reason, and it rules out fuzzy matching; Berkeley Lab likewise keeps its queue figures and EIA's side by side without matching them.

`Ruling:`

### 65. Severance tax calculator, gas: One rule for the energy in a thousand cubic feet of gas

- **Page:** Severance tax calculator, gas, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/severance
- **Question:** Gas is priced per MMBtu and taxed per thousand cubic feet (Mcf). The severance calculator treats one Mcf as one MMBtu; the Local impact tool uses EIA's heat content, 1.034 MMBtu per Mcf. Should both use one rule?
- **Now:** Each page keeps its rule. One well's 46,500 Mcf of Texas gas at USD 2.99: USD 10,427.63 on the severance calculator.
- **Other choice:** One rule; the branch moves the severance calculator to 1.034 MMBtu per Mcf: USD 10,782.16. New Mexico's taxes, a percent of value, move the same way (USD 11,039.38 to 11,414.72); Louisiana's gas tax is per Mcf and stays USD 7,040.10; oil does not move.
- **Change:** +354.53, +3.40 percent on every gas tax that is a percent of value.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-severance-3](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-severance-3). The branch moves the calculator only; the Lease tab and the lease finder still price one Mcf as one MMBtu and would move with a ruling to switch.
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **switch**. A published figure checks it: EIA's heat content of natural gas is 1,034 Btu a cubic foot (2024), so an Mcf holds 1.034 MMBtu. The calculator's 1.000 has no source, and the two pages now give different taxes for the same gas.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, severance tax](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-severance-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, severance tax](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-severance-2026-11-17.md)

### 67. What a battery earns, the signed method note: Should the signed battery note follow the newest months

- **Page:** What a battery earns, the signed method note, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/cost-of-power/battery
- **Question:** A test holds the battery page's signed method note to the twelve months it was signed on (October 2025 to September 2026), while the page itself already moves with the data. Should the note follow the data too?
- **Now:** The note's default case: USD 81.40 per kW a year, October 2025 to September 2026 (signed 14 October 2026). The page on 17 November shows USD 80.34 per kW, November 2025 to October 2026.
- **Other choice:** The note states the newest twelve months: USD 80.34 per kW, November 2025 to October 2026, the page's own figure.
- **Change:** The note's figure -1.06 USD per kW, -1.3 percent. No figure of the page moves: it already follows the data.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-battery-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-battery-1)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [battery-1-window-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/battery-1-window-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No recommendation: the measurement and the build give this figure differently (see the foot). To weigh: the page and its signed note disagree today by USD 1.06 per kW; if the note follows the data, its figures change after it was signed, and the hourly download and its Stata file must be rebuilt by the data machine each month.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, battery page](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-battery-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, battery page](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-battery-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** The publishers do not keep a signed note beside a page that moves, so none makes this exact choice; what they do with newer data is issue a new dated statement and keep the old one. Lazard's research page for *Levelized Cost of Energy+* (https://www.lazard.com/research-insights/levelized-cost-of-energyplus/) lists the 2026, June 2025, June 2024 and April 2023 editions side by side, each with its own figures (the two newest read today). The Annual Technology Baseline is issued by year (the 2025 pages at https://atb.nlr.gov/electricity/2025/utility-scale_battery_storage), and both market monitors report by year (Potomac Economics's 2025 report of June 2026; CAISO's Department of Market Monitoring's 2025 annual report of June 2026). The other choice is built that way: a dated paragraph with the newest twelve months, the signed figures kept for the record.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** His ruling of 12 October 2026 in the roadmap's "Rulings" (`docs/roadmap.md`): "the battery headline counts whole months only". By it the page's twelve months moved to November 2025 to October 2026 once October was whole. No ruling was found on whether a note may change after it is signed (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap and the page standard for signed, sign-off and test 178). In the file of 11 October the lines on this (B13, the downloads go stale when the window moves; M1 and M26, tests frozen so a landing is not set against the day's data) are sessions' recommendations, and that file says no ruling is made in it.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/battery_earns_algorithm.md`, section 11.2 (session 263), states the rule for its own figures: they are of the table on 14 October 2026, and "when October 2026 is complete every one moves", the script, the table and the review pack changed together. Its opening paragraph dates the default case to the tables as retrieved on 5 October 2026. Its "Sign-off" lists what a signature accepts (the model, the assumptions, the 90 percent month, the scenarios, the decisions, the rulings): rules, not a twelve-month window; and on main today the block's "Signed off by" line is empty, and 14 October 2026 is, in the note, the day of the table its figures were checked on (section 11.2), not a signature's date.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **switch**. The tool's own method note decided it: section 11.2 says its figures move when October 2026 is whole, changed together with the script and the pack, and October is whole (the page already shows it under Samuel's ruling of 12 October); the hourly download and its do-file must then be rebuilt by the data machine for each new window, which the branch says and does not do.

`Ruling:`

- **Page:** The project map, the Projects tab, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/map
- **Question:** The map draws the projects waiting in the connection queues and the datacenters, with their names and megawatts, from its own copy of two tables that are held for a license elsewhere on the site (the datacenter tracker shows no facility at all). Should the map's copy be held too, or the hold lifted for those rows? (This one question is listed twice in the rulings list, under the map and under the datacenter tracker.)
- **Now:** Nothing held on the map beyond two grids' queues. The map's file (built 16 and 17 November): 3,684 queue projects (768,296.0 MW) and 278 datacenters (28,254.0 MW), each with its name, operator and megawatts; 35,035 points in all.
- **Other choice:** Held. As bare points: the 3,684 queue projects lose 768,296.0 MW and the 278 datacenters 28,254.0 MW from every sum and hover; the count of points stays. As no layer: the tab draws the 31,073 generating units alone. The branch holds each table exactly as the license file does: New England's queue rows (422 on the builder's tables) counted and not drawn, and the datacenter layer counted and not drawn. Or the hold is lifted: nothing on the map moves.
- **Change:** Up to 3,962 points lose their name and megawatts, or leave the tab (-11.3 percent of its points).
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-map-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-map-1), [wip/alt-datacenters-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-datacenters-1). The first holds both layers as the license file holds their tables; the second, built for the datacenter tracker's copy of the question, keeps the 278 datacenters as bare points ("The map holds 278 datacenters, no MW stated"). Lifting the hold is not built. The first shows only after the data machine runs its builder.
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [datacenters-1-map-layer-held-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/datacenters-1-map-layer-held-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **switch**. Switch to holding the map's copy as the license file holds each table. The owner's rules of 16 November 2026 say a hold is never lifted or worked around, and the page standard says a held value is drawn blank with "license needed"; today the map sends the names, operators and megawatts of rows the datacenter tracker holds back.

### 69. The warehouse's table of daily power exchange between grid areas (read by the chat and the downloads; no page draws it): May EIA's six-month file fill two thin months of 2025

- **Page:** The warehouse's table of daily power exchange between grid areas (read by the chat and the downloads; no page draws it), https://erw-flame.vercel.app/ask
- **Question:** EIA's service answers November and December 2025 with most days missing. EIA's own six-month file holds them, and matches the service on 96.3 percent of the days both hold. May the six-month file fill the gap?
- **Now:** Filled, with the difference written in the method note: November and December 2025 hold 3,846 area-days (November 1,854, December 1,992), 2,779 of them from the six-month file.
- **Other choice:** Not filled: the months stay as the service answers them, 1,067 area-days (November 333, December 734); no area's November or December is whole.
- **Change:** 2,779 area-days removed, -72 percent of those two months' rows.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-site-data-machine-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-site-data-machine-1)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: both sources are EIA's; 6,811 of 7,074 area-days compared are equal to the MWh, and the rest differ by EIA's later revisions, by a few MWh except in one area; without the fill, no area's last two months of 2025 can be summed.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, the data machine's tables](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-site-data-machine-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, the data machine's tables](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-site-data-machine-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** One publisher stands on both sides, and its own account of the two routes could not be read today. EIA publishes Form EIA-930 through its data service and through six-month files of the same form; the page that describes them (the Hourly Electric Grid Monitor's "About" page, https://www.eia.gov/electricity/gridmonitor/about) is drawn by script and gave no text, and the form's instructions sit under a path EIA's robots file disallows: not read today, so this decides nothing. What was read: EIA, Today in Energy, "EIA's Hourly Electric Grid Monitor provides timely data about electricity usage patterns" (6 April 2020, https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/detail.php?id=43295): total interchange is published "with a one-day lag"; the article says nothing on revisions or on the two routes.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** Ruling 1 of 6 November 2026 (`docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`): a further part of a federal filing the warehouse already holds may be pulled, because "the source is the same publisher" and the hold on new sources does not bar it. The six-month file is the same publisher's same form, and the warehouse already reads those files for other tables (`docs/methods/eia930_all_history.md`, `docs/methods/eia930_region_hourly.md`). The non-negotiables (`CLAUDE.md`, rules 1 and 5): real data only, never filled in or made up, and every number traces to its source; the filled rows are EIA's own published hours, summed, and each row's source names the six-month file. Nothing on this fill itself in `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md` or the roadmap's "Rulings" (searched: interchange, six-month, fill, gap).
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/grid_network.md`, the paragraph "November and December 2025 in `eia930_daily_total_interchange`" (session 3941): the connector asks EIA's service again for a month it answered short, and takes each area's day the service still lacks from EIA's six-month file only when every hour of that day is in the file with a number; on July to October 2025 the two agreed to the MWh on 6,811 of 7,074 area-days, the rest differing by EIA's later revisions; 2,779 days were filled, each row's source the file; a regional total the file does not hold stays absent. `docs/methods/eia930_daily_total_interchange.md` (session 2242) records the 46 days the service returned empty.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **keep**. The method note states the rule and its test (a day is taken from EIA's own six-month file only when whole, each row marked with that source, the difference measured and written down), and Samuel's ruling of 6 November 2026 treats a further file of a publisher already held as the same source.

`Ruling:`

### 73. Price board: Blank a stale row's 7-day and 30-day figures too

- **Page:** Price board, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/board
- **Question:** On a row marked stale (its latest value is older than the board allows), the daily moves and the 12-month average are already blanked. Should the 7-day and 30-day averages and the 30-day low and high be blanked too?
- **Now:** Drawn, each saying on hover how many days it covers. On the file of 17 November, 116 stale rows draw a 7-day average, among them Texas's and the Southwest Power Pool's real-time peak rows (4 days old).
- **Other choice:** Blanked, each reading "stale" with the row's age on hover. 157 fresh rows keep theirs.
- **Change:** 464 figures go blank (four cells on each of 116 rows).
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-board-2](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-board-2)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: the row is already marked stale and half its cells are blank, so the other choice treats the row one way; against it, the page standard says an upgrade drops nothing the page had, and each of the 464 figures states its days on hover today.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, price board](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-board-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, price board](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-board-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** One publisher read, on a like case only. EIA, "Today in Energy: Daily Prices" (https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/prices.php, the page of 18 November 2026): every cell of its futures table reads "NA" where no current settlement is held; EIA prints no older figure in the place of a current one. The page carries no 7-day or 30-day average at all, so it does not make this choice for a window that ended days ago. The exchanges' pages were not read today (ICE's terms bar robots; CME's site did not answer).
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** His ruling of 18 October 2026, as `docs/methods/price_board.md` records it (decision 5 of the page's list): a row is marked stale past 3, 10, 17, 132 or 135 days by its step; the ruling sets when a row is stale, and the blanking of the moves came after it (session 1608, 6 November 2026). The page standard (rule 9 of `queue/done/302-day-16-november-oldlaptop-large-load-on-the-site.md`) pulls two ways: an upgrade "drops nothing the page had", and explanations belong in hover tips, which is where each of the 464 figures states its days today; but a value that may not be shown is drawn as a blank cell with its reason on hover, which is what the other choice does. None found on these four cells (searched the two rulings files and the roadmap for stale, 7-day and average).
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/price_board.md`, "The columns of every row" and "The moves: the year to date, and none on a stale row": on a stale row the moves, the 12-month average and the day's high and low read "stale", because a figure measured from a series past its threshold "is not shown as current"; the 7-day and 30-day averages and the 30-day low to high carry no such limit and state their count on hover. The note blanks one window mean that ends on the stale date (twelve months) and draws two others (7 and 30 days); it gives no reason for the difference. The board's read (`docs/reviews/board-read-2026-11-16.md`, row 14) left it as a default for this ruling.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. The method note blanks the 12-month average on a stale row yet lists the 7-day and 30-day figures as drawn, the page standard supports both a kept figure with its hover and a blank cell with its reason, and EIA's page (which prints "NA" for a missing close) has no window averages to compare.

`Ruling:`

### 74. The Attention and Friction indices: No Friction Index under ten stories

- **Page:** The Attention and Friction indices, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/attention
- **Question:** The Friction Index is the share of a week's stories about a place that report opposition. With few stories it jumps: Virginia's datacenters read 75.00 from 3 of 4 stories. Should a cell with fewer than ten stories print its counts and no index?
- **Now:** Every index printed, with its counts beside it: 461 cells of a sector, a place and a week.
- **Other choice:** Counts only under ten stories ("3 of 4 stories report opposition: fewer than 10, so no index"). 363 of the 461 indices go, every one a state's cell: 293 of them read 0, 26 read 100 and 18 read 50. In the newest week 42 of 60 go. The national cells and the rows over all weeks keep theirs; the story counts and the Attention Index do not move.
- **Change:** 363 indices removed, 78.7 percent of them; their counts stay.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-attention-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-attention-1)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [attention-1-small-cells-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/attention-1-small-cells-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: 337 of the 363 read exactly 0, 50 or 100, the values a handful of stories gives; against that, the page standard says an upgrade drops nothing the page had, and each index already shows its counts. Ten stories is the number the question names, not a tested threshold.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, attention indices](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-attention-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branch, attention indices](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-attention-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** Not settled by what could be read. The one publisher read today that prints figures on small groups shows them with their counts and a warning, and does not blank them: Berkeley Lab, "Queued Up: 2026 Edition" (the slide deck, https://eta-publications.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2026-06/queued_up_2026_edition.pdf), slide 51, prints the number of projects behind each region's figure by year "to provide transparency", and slide 42, note 4, says where a year's sample is small. That is what the page does now (each index with its counts beside it). The federal standard for when a share is not printed at all, the National Center for Health Statistics' presentation standards for proportions, was not read today: cdc.gov refused the request for its robots file (HTTP 403), so no claim is taken from it. No publisher of a news index with a floor of ten stories was found.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on a smallest count for an index (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's rulings and the page standard for fewer, floor, minimum, stories and index). The page standard, rule 9 of the chain file of 16 November 2026, cuts both ways: an upgrade "drops nothing the page had" (363 indices would go, their counts staying), and a page is to be "dense, informative, usable", which does not say at what count a share stops informing.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/attention.md` has no rule on a smallest number of stories. Its "Limits, said plainly" says the Friction Index counts stories, not events, and that a state is set for only a small share of stories. Two other tools of the site do set a floor, each in its own note: `/queues` writes a share only from 20 requests, since under that one project moves it by five points or more (`docs/methods/interconnection_queue_summary.md`), and the deal tracker gives a median only from three deals (`docs/methods/deal_tracker.md`, section 7.3). Neither floor is ten, and neither note covers this page.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. Looked for a publisher's or a ruling's floor for printing a share and found none that fits: Berkeley Lab prints small groups with their counts, the federal standard could not be read, the site's own floors are 20 and 3 and not 10, and the attention note is silent, so whether to blank a small cell and at what count is not decided by the evidence.

`Ruling:`

### 75. The datacenter tracker: Show the datacenter rows whose every source is public

- **Page:** The datacenter tracker, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/datacenters
- **Question:** The table of datacenter facilities is held whole for a license, because some rows rest on news sources: no reader sees any facility. Should the rows whose every source is public be shown, each with its license?
- **Now:** Held whole. 274 facilities are counted; 21 of them state megawatts, 28,254 MW.
- **Other choice:** The public rows shown: 210 of the 274 rest only on public sources (the operators' own location pages, the Texas grid operator) and would appear; 3 of them state megawatts, 882 MW. The other 64, which hold 27,372 of the 28,254 MW, rest on at least one news source and stay held.
- **Change:** 210 rows appear; the megawatt figures stay almost all held (882 of 28,254 MW shown).
- **Branch:** not built: the hold stands (the owner's rule of 16 November: a hold is never lifted or worked around).
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [datacenters-1-map-layer-held-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/datacenters-1-map-layer-held-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. Only Samuel can open part of a held table, and no ruling covers this one. To weigh: his ruling of 6 November 2026 on the large-load table set a license row by row (public where the row's source is a public record, held where a host's terms ask permission), which is the same idea; here the list would come back nearly whole while the megawatts, which rest on news sources, would not.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, datacenter tracker](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-datacenters-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, datacenter tracker](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-datacenters-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** None applies. This is a question of license, what the ERW may show of rows read from other hosts, and not a counting choice a publisher makes; no publisher was looked up for it. It is Samuel's call unless one of his own rulings decides it.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** Ruling 4 of 6 November 2026 (`docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`), on the large-load tariff table: public, row by row; a row whose source is a public regulatory record is public, and a row whose only source is a host whose terms ask written permission is internal "until the permission is on record", its cell blanked with a note that a license is needed. Also the ruling of 12 October 2026 (`docs/roadmap.md`), which the tracker's decision 12 names as the ground of its hold: NYISO's and SPP's tables stay held until their licenses are answered. Applied here, ruling 4's test does not give the 210 rows the list counts. Those rows rest on the operators' own location pages, and the license file records every one of the nine operators as "needs written permission" (`docs/licenses.md`, reads 32 to 40, read again on 13 November 2026: Amazon Web Services, Google, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle and Vantage by a sentence of their terms; Digital Realty, Equinix and Switch not read, permission by default). The measurement took each source's flag from `warehouse/metadata/sources.csv`, which marks those lists public; the verdicts are the stricter record.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/datacenter_tracker.md`, "The hold" and decision 12: the table is held whole since 21 October 2026 because most rows rest on news stories "or on operators' own pages, whose terms ask for permission"; under the hold the page already draws what rests on public sources: the queue sites (two, both ERCOT's), ERCOT's section and the power side. `docs/methods/datacenter_facilities.md`, "Held by row": the site can hold the table row by row once the data machine writes each row's license; counted that way on 20 October there were 2 public rows, both from ERCOT's queue. `docs/methods/datacenter_operator_sites.md`, "License", gives the nine verdicts.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **keep**. Samuel's ruling 4 of 6 November decided it by its own test: a row whose only source is a host that asks written permission stays held, the operators' own pages are such hosts in the license file, so the 210 rows are not public rows, and the few rows that do rest on a public record (ERCOT's queue sites) are already drawn under the hold.

`Ruling:`

### 77. Price board and the price pages: Buy access to held prices (PJM membership, an ICE license)

- **Page:** Price board and the price pages, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/board
- **Question:** Should the ERW buy access to held prices: a PJM membership, or a redistribution license from the exchange ICE, at USD 25,000 to 30,000 a year?
- **Now:** Nothing bought. 88 rows of the board are held for a license: New York 25, New England 21, PJM 13, S&P Global or Platts 15 (daily gas hubs, coal), ICE 3 (European gas, European carbon allowances, renewable certificates), others 11.
- **Other choice:** Bought. A PJM membership opens 13 rows; an ICE license 3. The values are not held for most of them, so the cells that would be drawn are known and their figures are not.
- **Change:** Up to 13 rows (PJM) or 3 rows (ICE) gain figures, for USD 25,000 to 30,000 a year.
- **Branch:** not built: it is a purchase, not code.
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. A purchase is Samuel's alone to decide; no published figure, ruling, page standard or method note bears on it. To weigh: USD 25,000 to 30,000 a year opens 13 or 3 of the 88 held rows; New York's and New England's 46 rows need those operators' own permission, not a purchase.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, price board](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-board-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, price board](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-board-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** Not a choice other publishers make for the ERW; what there is to know is the price, and it was not read again today (ICE's terms of use bar robots and data gathering, so its fee page was not asked; PJM's page was not asked). The repository's own record of the publishers' pages, `docs/licenses_costs.md` (read 13 November 2026), differs from this entry's figure: PJM's membership is USD 2,500 a year for an associate member and USD 5,000 for a voting member (plus USD 2,000 and USD 1,500 once for a market participant); the USD 25,000 to 30,000 is ICE's redistribution license for its US futures exchange alone (USD 10,000 for its European exchange, USD 3,000 for public display). The same record says two things are not settled: whether an associate member is a "Member" who may republish under PJM's data license, and what ICE charges for the over-the-counter hub prices EIA republishes, for which ICE names no price.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None decides a purchase (searched the two rulings files, the roadmap and the page standard for buy, purchase, membership and license). What stands near it: the roadmap's "Holds" names "New sources", and PJM's 13 rows hold no values today, so a membership would draw nothing until a pull of PJM's prices is also allowed and built; rule 5 of 16 November 2026 says a license hold is never lifted or worked around, and a license bought from the publisher is neither. The defaults list (`docs/reviews/rulings-defaults-2026-11-17.md`) carries "no letter sent, nothing bought" as the default, which is not a ruling.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/price_board.md`, "What the board cannot show, and why, publisher by publisher": PJM's rows need PJM's data license and an account; the European gas and carbon rows need ICE Endex's or LSEG's license; New York's and New England's rows need a written permission, not a purchase. Its section "What waits for the owner" lists each as standing until its license. The note states what would supply each row and no rule on buying.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. A purchase is Samuel's call alone and no ruling of his decides it; the one thing found is that the repository's record of 13 November puts PJM's membership at USD 2,500 to 5,000 a year, not the USD 25,000 to 30,000 the entry gives, which is ICE's fee.

`Ruling:`

### 78. What a battery earns, the fold under the income table: Keep the monthly fold on the page or in the download alone

- **Page:** What a battery earns, the fold under the income table, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/cost-of-power/battery
- **Question:** Under the income table a fold gives, for each of twelve months, the days held and the price spread at the points where batteries sit. Should those rows stay on the page, or be in the download alone?
- **Now:** The fold is on the page: 72 cells, six figures for each of 12 months.
- **Other choice:** No fold; the 72 figures become six new columns of the monthly download. The twelve-month figures above it do not change.
- **Change:** 72 cells leave the page and stay in the download.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-battery-2](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-battery-2)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [battery-2-fold-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/battery-2-fold-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No recommendation: the measurement and the build count this differently (see the foot). To weigh: the page standard says an upgrade drops nothing the page had, which points to keeping the fold; the rulings list cites a ruling of 12 October that moved monthly rows to the download, which points the other way and is not in the two rulings files read here.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, battery page](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-battery-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, battery page](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-battery-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** The publishers do not make this choice: a fold on a web page is not something a report has. Where they keep month-by-month rows was read for what it is worth. Potomac Economics, *2025 State of the Market Report for the ERCOT Electricity Markets* (June 2026, https://www.potomaceconomics.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/2025-State-of-the-Market-Report-for-ERCOT.pdf): the year's figures are in the text, and the month-by-month table ("Market at a Glance Monthly", Table A5) is in the appendix. CAISO's Department of Market Monitoring, *2025 Annual Report on Market Issues and Performance* (June 2026, https://www.caiso.com/documents/2025-annual-report-on-market-issues-and-performance.pdf): months are drawn in charts (for example pages 61 and 62), not listed beside the year's battery figures (pages 118 and 119, by quarter). Neither publishes a storage node spread by month, so no published figure checks the 72 cells.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** A ruling on this page and on this kind of row. The roadmap's "Rulings" (`docs/roadmap.md`, 12 October 2026, the chain prompt of sessions 208 and 209): the battery page is one page, three questions, "nothing collapsed"; the fold "Every month" goes into the CSV download, and the folds "Stress days" and "What this model cannot see" go. This is the ruling of 12 October that the ranked list could not find in the two rulings files: it is in the roadmap, not in `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md` or `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`. Pulling the other way, the page standard (rule 9 of `queue/done/302-day-16-november-oldlaptop-large-load-on-the-site.md`, 16 November 2026): an upgrade is made to the page itself and drops nothing the page had. Under the other choice the 72 figures leave the page and stay in the page's own download, as the monthly rows did under his ruling.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/battery_earns_algorithm.md`, section 12, decision 10, the list the page draws at its foot and a test holds word for word: one page, three questions, nothing collapsed, "the monthly rows are the CSV download" (session 208; the owner's ruling of 12 October 2026); the same section says the decisions block is drawn open because this page has nothing collapsed. Section 4.9: since session 208 the fold "Every month" is gone and its rows are `months.csv`. Section 11.3: the fold in question was added by session 355 on 20 October 2026, eight days after the ruling, with each month's node figures in a fold and the twelve months' means on the page; the means stay under either choice.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **switch**. Samuel's ruling of 12 October 2026 for this page decided it: nothing collapsed, and monthly rows live in the CSV download, which the page's own decisions list still states and which the fold of 20 October went against; his page standard of 16 November pulls the other way (the cells leave the page), and drawing the 72 cells open on the page, which would meet both, is not one of the two choices.

### 80. Where power contracts are being struck, capacity prices: Show capacity prices converted to USD per kW-month

- **Page:** Where power contracts are being struck, capacity prices, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/contracts
- **Question:** Capacity prices are listed as filed, in three units (dollars per MW a month, per MW a day, per kW a month). Should each priced row also show one converted figure, in USD per kW a month?
- **Now:** As filed, three units, no conversion. The second quarter of 2026: 33 rows in dollars per MW-month (median 4,941.00), 15 per MW-day (median 164.70), 2 per kW-month (median 5.75).
- **Other choice:** A second figure beside each priced row. The second quarter of 2026: 50 figures added, median USD 5.01 per kW-month (1.83 to 15.45). The first quarter: 92 added, median 6.97; its largest, USD 8,845 per kW-month, is a row filed in the wrong unit, which the conversion exposes.
- **Change:** 50 figures added in the opening quarter; no figure that stands moves.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-contracts-5](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-contracts-5)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [contracts-5-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/contracts-5-1280.png), [contracts-5-head-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/contracts-5-head-1280.png); the page today: [contracts-5-default-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/contracts-5-default-1280.png), [contracts-5-head-default-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/contracts-5-head-default-1280.png)
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: without one unit a reader cannot compare two capacity prices; the conversion needs a day-to-month factor (30.4375 days a month here; 30, or the month's own days, are other choices), which the ruling would also fix.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, contracts](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-contracts-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, contracts](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-contracts-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** FERC, Electric Quarterly Report Data Dictionary, Version 3.5, issued 23 November 2020 (https://www.ferc.gov/sites/default/files/2020-11/Data_Dictionary_V3_5_Clean.pdf; whether a later version is in force was not read today). In the contract file the price is kept in the unit the contract states: Field 38 (page 9) is the measure "stated in the contract", and Appendix F (page 23) lists dollars per kW-month, per MW-month and per MW-day as three allowed units, with no conversion among them; Appendix A (page 16) defines capacity as charged on a dollars per kW or per MW basis. FERC does put capacity prices in one unit elsewhere: in the transaction file, Field 68 (page 14), the standardized price, is in dollars per MW-month for capacity. That figure is filed by the seller, its unit is the MW-month and not the kW-month, and the dictionary gives no number of days in a month for turning a daily price into a monthly one. The grid operators' own capacity price units were not read today.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on converting a filed price (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's rulings and the page standard for convert, unit, capacity price and kW-month). The ruling of 12 October 2026 (`docs/roadmap.md`) that every assumption shows its default with its source bears on the factor: 30.4375 days a month would be an assumption with no published source.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/contracts.md`, decision 7 ("price-as-filed"), states the rule: a price is the rate and its units when the filer gave a number, and "no price is filled in, converted or estimated". `docs/methods/eqr_terms.md`, "A price from the words", keeps a figure read from words in its own unit and says a capacity price per kW and month is never turned into dollars per megawatt-hour.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **keep**. The method note decided it: the page's rule is the price as filed, none converted, which is also how FERC's contract file holds it; where FERC does give capacity one unit it is the seller who files it, in dollars per MW-month, and no published factor exists for the days in a month the conversion needs.

`Ruling:`

### 81. Automated Analysis, the findings library: Withdraw three findings cards built from internal tables

- **Page:** Automated Analysis, the findings library, https://erw-flame.vercel.app/research
- **Question:** Three of the library's 14 findings cards were built from tables the ERW holds as internal: one from New England's prices, two from the Texas commission's transmission tables. Their files can be reached by address. Should the three be withdrawn?
- **Now:** Left as they are; a test keeps the home page and this page from opening to visitors while they wait.
- **Other choice:** Withdrawn: the library holds 11 cards; the three cards' 9 callouts, 49 numbers, 6 chart series and 9 downloads leave it, and their addresses answer "not found". Their download files would still be reachable by address unless they are deleted, which the repository's rules forbid without a ruling.
- **Change:** 3 of 14 cards removed (-21.4 percent), 49 numbers with them.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-research-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-research-1)
- **Screenshots:** the other choice: [research-1-cards-withdrawn-1280.png](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives/research-1-cards-withdrawn-1280.png); the page today: screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. Whether a finding computed from an internal table is itself held is not settled by a ruling (the same question is open for Louisiana's tax in Local impact). To weigh: the owner's rule that a hold is never worked around points to withdrawing; the cards print results computed from those tables, not the tables' rows. A ruling to withdraw would also have to say whether the download files go and how a reader's past request for a withdrawn card is shown.
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, Automated Analysis](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-research-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, Automated Analysis](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-research-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** None applies. This is a question of license, what the ERW may show of results computed from tables it holds as internal, and not a choice a publisher makes; no publisher was looked up for it. It is Samuel's call unless one of his own rulings decides it.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** Ruling 6a of 19 October 2026, one of his rulings on section 9 of the Research pack, recorded in `docs/reviews/packs/research.md` ("Section 9's outcomes") and in `docs/methods/automated_analysis_findings.md` ("The Research tool, its form and its figures"): the figures of NYISO and ISO-NE on the findings cards read "license needed" from 21 October, and "the cards stay". That is this question for one of the three cards (`gas_sets_price`, built in part from New England's prices), already ruled: the card is not withdrawn, its held figures are blanked. The page standard, rule 9 of the chain file of 16 November 2026, says the same of any tool: a held or internal value is not left off, its cell is drawn blank with "license needed". The owner's rule 5 of 16 November (a hold is never lifted or worked around) forbids showing the held figures, not keeping the card. No ruling names the Texas commission's transmission tables behind the other two cards.
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/automated_analysis_findings.md`, "License": a card and its downloads carry the most restrictive license of their inputs; the card built on New England's hourly prices publishes yearly aggregates only. `docs/methods/siting.md`, "The cards' files": the two siting cards are built from `texas_transmission_matrix`, an internal table; the tab draws that part as "license needed", but the cards' CSV files still carry the column and are served by address; a test keeps `/` and `/research` closed while the owner's ruling is awaited.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **keep**. Samuel's ruling 6a of 19 October 2026 decided it: a card with figures of a held source stays and those figures read "license needed", so the three are not withdrawn; applying that same ruling to the figures and the CSV column that rest on the Texas commission's table is a step neither choice takes, and the fate of those download files is still his to say.
