### 129. Supply and trade, "Changes outside the five years' range": Leave near-zero series out of the range list

- **Page:** Supply and trade, "Changes outside the five years' range", https://erw-flame.vercel.app/supply
- **Question:** The list ranks each series by how far its latest value sits outside its own five-year range. A series that is almost zero leads the list, because its range is almost zero. Should a series below a stated floor be left out?
- **Now:** Every series: 21 lines, led by a small category of gas export (latest 0.004 billion cubic feet a day, 31.76 times its range out). The next line is at 1.85.
- **Other choice:** A floor of 0.05 billion cubic feet a day leaves that one line out: 20 lines, led by US distillate fuel stocks (103.33 million barrels, 1.85 times its range out).
- **Change:** One line removed; the list's first line changes.
- **Branch:** [wip/alt-supply-1](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/tree/wip/alt-supply-1)
- **Screenshots:** screenshot not taken yet
- **Recommendation:** **no basis to recommend**. No allowed ground decides it. To weigh: the line that leads the list today is an artefact of a range near zero; the form of the floor is part of the ruling (a floor stated as a share of the series' own average leaves nothing out, since that average is as small).
- **Figures from:** [the measurement, supply and trade](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-impact-supply-2026-11-17.md), [the record of the branches, supply and trade](https://github.com/SamuelEnrique/erw/blob/main/docs/reviews/ruling-alternatives-supply-2026-11-17.md)
- **Evidence (looked up on 19 November 2026):**
  - **Published practice:** The publishers do not make this choice: none read today publishes a list that ranks series by how far a change stands outside its own five-year range, so none sets a floor for one. The nearest practice read is of another kind. EIA, Electric Power Monthly, Table ES1.A (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/monthly/epm_table_grapher.php?t=table_es1a), the notes under the table: a cell is printed as "NM", not meaningful, where its relative standard error is large; that is a rule about sampling error, not about a series near zero, and it marks the cell where it stands instead of leaving the row out. EIA's weekly storage report, which sets a level against its five-year range, was not read today.
  - **Samuel's own rulings on a like question:** None found on a floor for the list (searched `docs/state/rulings_2026-10-11.md`, `docs/state/rulings_2026-11-06.md`, the roadmap's "Rulings" and the page standard for: floor, range, five years, near zero, list). Two standing rules bear on it without deciding. The page standard of 16 November 2026: an upgrade "drops nothing the page had"; here the series keeps its own row in the gas trade table and only its line in the list would go. The roadmap ruling of 12 October 2026: every assumption shows its default with its source; the branch's floor of 0.05 billion cubic feet a day was chosen by the session and has no source (the record of the branch lists it under decisions made without him).
  - **The tool's own method note:** `docs/methods/supply_and_trade.md`, "The surprise": the list holds the series whose newest change is outside the five years' changes, ordered by how far the change stands from the five-year average change, "measured in the width" of those five changes. The note sets no floor and does not speak of a series whose width is near zero; its replication says only that a series without all five years is never marked outside.
- **Recommendation after the evidence:** **no basis to recommend**. Looked for and not found: a publisher's floor for such a list, or a ruling or method rule on a series whose five-year range is near zero; the line that leads the list is an artefact of dividing by a width near zero, but the only floor built has no source, and its form (a fixed quantity for one unit) is itself part of what Samuel would rule.

`Ruling:`

## Figures that do not agree

Each of the twelve entries below gave a figure one way in the measurement file of 17 November and another way in the record of the branch that built the other choice. Chain 503 traced each to its cause on 19 November 2026, from the files, the branch's code and test, the page's file or table, and the measurement's own script and printout, and recomputed the figure where the rows are on the machine. Each bullet keeps the two figures that disagreed and says which is right.

- **Entry 6, Shoulder hours, Texas by month:** the right figure: 7 Texas months fall to 0 MWh (February, March and November 2019, January 2020, and March, November and December 2021), not 8. What was wrong: the measurement file's summary line says 8 where its own printout lists 7; the branch's builder, run again on the table, gives 7 of the 22 months that change. The entry gives 7 (cause: a summary that miscounts its printout).
- **Entry 18, Should your family get an EV? (the game), California:** the right figures: saved a year USD 1,707 to 1,381 (-19.1 percent) and net present value USD 6,030 to 3,924 (-34.9 percent), the record of the branch's, on the price file the page reads. What was wrong: the measurement's USD 1,897 to 1,750 (-7.8 percent) and USD 7,258 to 6,312 (-13.0 percent) are on the price file of 15 October (California: electricity 33.61 cents of July, gasoline USD 6.227 a gallon of 5 October), which main replaced on 17 November (29.75 cents of August, gasoline USD 5.562 of 9 November). Both sets recompute exactly, each on its own file; the gasoline price moved as well as the electricity price, which is why the saving fell although power got cheaper (cause: a file rebuilt between the two readings).
- **Entry 48, Automated Analysis, "Today's chart" of 12 November 2026:** the right figure: 295,122 MWh, the margin the stored chart itself holds (295,122.3: the table's 1,060,503.7 less 765,381.4), as the record of the branch has it. What was wrong: the measurement's 295,123 MWh subtracts the two figures after each was rounded to a whole MWh (1,060,504 less 765,381). The entry prints neither (cause: a rounding).
- **Entry 53, What a datacenter pays, the All grids tab:** the right figures: the commission's rate USD 8.62 per MWh, Oncor's own 8.58 (-0.04, -0.5 percent) and CenterPoint's 6.92 (-1.70, -19.8 percent), the build's. What was wrong: the measurement's 7.83 is the commission's 2025 rate (USD 68.547301 per kW a year), the newest approved until the page's file was rebuilt on 16 November with the 2026 rate (USD 75.527270, 8.62 per MWh) marked approved; set beside Oncor's 8.58, which is the same on both files, it gave +0.75 (+9.6 percent), a gap between two years' rates and not between the commission and the utility (cause: a rate of the wrong year).
- **Entry 67, What a battery earns, the signed method note:** the right figure: USD 80.34 per kW (-1.3 percent on the signed 81.40), November 2025 to October 2026, the build's and the page's. What was wrong: the measurement's USD 79.07 (-2.9 percent) is on a copy of the table as of 21 October, holding 19 of October 2026's days (USD 4.94 per kW for the month, against 6.21 in the rows read on 17 November). Those newer rows hold 29 of October's 31 days: two days are left out for a missing price, so October is nearly whole, not whole (cause: a partial month).
- **Entry 78, What a battery earns, the fold under the income table:** the right figure: 72 cells, six figures for each of 12 months (the days held, the spread at the 10th percentile, the median and the 90th, the hub's spread and the share wider), as the page's code draws them. What was wrong: the measurement's 48 is not a count of the page: its script prints "4 figures for each of the 12 months" as fixed words, naming the days and the three spreads and leaving out the hub's spread and the share wider (cause: a count taken two ways).
- **Entry 89, The December 2022 winter storm (Elliott):** the right figure: 619,176 MWh for 23 December without temperature, as the page draws it; the exact value is 619,176.5, a half. What was wrong: nothing in either file's method. The site's own code computes 619,176.4999999993 and prints 619,176; the measurement's Python copy of the same rule computes 619,176.5000000002 and prints 619,177. Two programs whose last digits fall either side of the half, not one value rounded two ways (cause: a rounding).
- **Entry 113, Power prices, a hub's page:** the right figure: +407.82 USD per MWh for the largest real-time hour of Texas West less Houston (12 November, 03:00 UTC), as the page's own code draws it; the exact value is 407.815, a half cent (West's four prices average 520.2225, Houston's 112.4075). What was wrong: nothing in either method. The measurement's Python sum gives 407.81499999999994 and prints +407.81; adding the four prices one by one, as the page does, gives 407.81500000000005 and prints +407.82. The lowest, -217.67, agrees. The entry prints neither (cause: a rounding).
- **Entry 117, What a datacenter pays, a flexible load in Texas:** the right figure: USD 22,658,181 a year for 300 MW, at the 2026 rate (USD 75.527270 per kW), the build's and the page's. What was wrong: the measurement's USD 20,564,190 is at 2025's rate (USD 68.547301), the newest approved when it was taken; the page's file before 16 November held the 2026 rate as filed, and the file rebuilt that day holds it as approved (cause: a rate of the wrong year).
- **Entry 118, What a datacenter pays, California's connection steps:** the right figure: "60 to 90 days", which both documents print (the earlier on its page 11, the later on its page 25); neither prints 90 alone. The measurement's "60 to 90 days" is the printed sentence; the build's "90 days" is the table's field for the earlier document, which holds the upper end of the range. What was wrong: the table holds one sentence two ways. Its method note writes a range with its words and no length, which is why the later document's field is blank (not a reading error), and says the earlier row was left with 90 as first written. Nothing needs carrying: the later document states the range itself. A request to the data machine asks for one rule for both rows (cause: other: the table holds a printed range as its upper end in one row and as no length in the other).
- **Entry 119, Curtailment, Texas:** the right figure: three shares on the view, over three spans, so two would leave, as the measurement says: 6.23 percent (the head, 47 whole days), 6.05 percent (October 2026) and 5.91 percent (the "By day" line, over the days of the daily table). What was wrong: the build looked for the 5.91 in the page's file of 15 November, where it is not held: the page works it out from the daily table as it is drawn. The branch as built takes out October's 6.05 and leaves the "By day" share on the page, so it does not yet show the head's share alone (cause: a count taken two ways).
- **Entry 127, What a generator earns, a gas peaker:** the right figure: USD 2.38 million, the page's, for 100 MW at the Houston hub (USD 43.13 per kW over the twelve months); the page's own code gives 2.38 on that revenue. What was wrong: the measurement's USD 2.53 million discounts over 35 years, the project life of the download's default solar plant, where the page gives a gas peaker 30 years. Its guess that the page's revenue base differs is not the cause (cause: other: the project life of the wrong plant, 35 years for 30).
